  :                            69 
  :                            70 LIBRARY	$equ	LIBRARY_XDR
  :                            71 
  :                            72 .	this library is for a stream continuum
  :                            73 .	so nothing is set up to initialise
  :                            74 .	a field pointer of any kind
  :                            75 .	except it is a counted stream and must
  :                            76 .	do nothing if no bytes are available
  :                            77 .	and stop when it runs out of bytes
  :                            78 
  :                            79 .	this generation runs with the defaults
  :                            80 .	ASCII / UTF8, 8-bit byte
  :                            81 
  :                            82 .	the initialisation call READ_START
  :                            83 .	programs the staging registers
  :                            84 .	RDATAC+RDATA+Q to shift two bytes
  :                            85 .	per word from network buffers,
  :                            86 .	which store datalike this:
  :                            87 .	
  :                            88 .		_________________________________
  :                            89 .		| 0000 0000 XXXX xxxx XXXX xxxx |
  :                            90 .		|_______________________________|
  :                            91 
  :                            92 .	immediately on acquisition via register *q++
  :                            93 .	if bstream16r is on, RDATAC is decremented
  :                            94 .	from 24 to 16 and RDATA is shifted 8 bits
  :                            95 .	left:
  :                            96 
  :                            97 .		_________________________________
  :                            98 .		| XXXX xxxx XXXX xxxx 0000 0000 |
  :                            99 .		|_______________________________|
  :                            100 
  :                            101 
  :                            102 .	in contrast to 8 bit data in program data
  :                            103 .	which uses all 24 bits in the data word:
  :                            104 
  :                            105 .		_________________________________
  :                            106 .		| XXXX xxxx XXXX xxxx XXXX xxxx |
  :                            107 .		|_______________________________|
  :                            108 
  :                            109 .	the staging registers deliver or output
  :                            110 .	16 bits per word if PSR flags
  :                            111 
  :                            112 .		b$stream16r
  :                            113 .		b$stream16w
  :                            114 
  :                            115 .	are on, otherwise 24 bits per word
  :                            116 
  :                            117 
  :                            118 	$list	2
  :                            119 
  :                            120 .	binary and math library data character code transparent
  :                            121 
  :                            122 $(0:0:LIBRARY)

  :                            41 
  :                            42 .	these are used in conjunction with
  :                            43 .	hard TCP / RPC / XDR / which handle
  :                            44 .	segments fragmented over IP plus messages
  :                            45 .	fragmented over segments with record
  :                            46 .	marks
  :                            47 
  :                            48 .	external data is 24-bit in application
  :                            49 .	space typically in scratch registers or
  :                            50 .	internal stack

  :                            55 f2_ieee754_64
00:006041 3D0000              +56 	fret	0
  :                            57 
  :                            58 .	below this line routines are available
  :                            59 .	for very simple UDP socket user programs
  :                            60 .	which access the 16-bit data in socket
  :                            61 .	buffers
  :                            62 
  :                            63 .	user datagrams have to be very short or DNF
  :                            64 .	so that no-fragments is assured for these
  :                            65 .	routines to be used safely

  :                            41 .	if the acquired mantissa fits in 72 bits use this conversion
  :                            42 .	because this should be integer accurate if it is correct code
  :                            43 
  :                            44 .	the technique of adding together two overlapping numbers
  :                            45 .	forces a small inaccuracy through hardware rounding. That
  :                            46 .	can't be accepted if the mantissa was 100% representable
  :                            47 .	in the first place

  :                            215 	$list		2
  :                            216 
  :                            217 	$if		FPXFPP
  :                            218: 	$include	../test.msm/fpxfpp
  :                            1 
  :                            2 .	routines called from macros fpx[D]
  :                            3 .	push and unpack 48-bit float to 96-bit
  :                            4 .	from storage to internal stack
  :                            5 
  :                            6 .	routines called from macro fpp[D]
  :                            7 .	pop and pack 96-bit float to 48-bit
  :                            8 .	from internal stack to storage
  :                            9 
  :                            10 .	macros fpx[D] fpp[D] call these routines
  :                            11 .	to remodel withdrawn instructions fpx fpp
  :                            12 .	to allow future decisions on data formats
  :                            13 
  :                            14 .	floating computation is 96-bit ( or 192-bit )
  :                            15 .	in the internal stack = SIMD accumulators
  :                            16  
  :                            17 .	or else floating computation is in
  :                            18 .	fixed accumulators a:b:6:7 [ 8:9:10:11 ]
  :                            19 
  :                            20 .	48-bit floating formats are storage only
  :                            21 .	in variants s.11.36 and [D] s.15.32
  :                            22 
  :                            23 .	SIMD 48-bit floating operation has been prototyped
  :                            24 .	with a view to getting 50 * 48-bit floating values
  :                            25 .	alternative to 25 * 96-bit values
  :                            26 .	in the internal stack = SIMD destination registers.
  :                            27 .	Follow-through would depend on actual purpose
  :                            28 
  :                            29 .	__fpp[D] rounds with fp$guard register value
  :                            30 
  :                            31 .	on truncation of significant exponent bits
  :                            32 .	from the computational 23-bit exponent
  :                            33 .	__fpp[D] pops 48-bit negative zero
  :                            34 
  :                            35 .	discarded bits follow the midpoint position.
  :                            36 .	If any discarded bits are equal to the
  :                            37 .	value at midpoint = midpoint_magnitude XOR sign
  :                            38 .	the result is unusable and __fpp[D] stores -0.0
  :                            39 
  :                            40 .	bit after sign is exponent midpoint
  :                            41 .	always retained in compressed floating values
  :                            42 
  :                            43 .	0.5 and above	sign.midpoint = 0.1.discarded.retained : mantissa...
  :                            44 .	< 0.5 		sign.midpoint = 0.0.discarded.retained : mantissa...
  :                            45 
  :                            46 .	$ masmx -lnke
  :                            47 .	00:000000  400000 800000 000000 000000	+1  0.5
  :                            48 .	00:000004  BFFFFF 7FFFFF FFFFFF FFFFFF	+2  -0.5
  :                            49 .	00:000008  3FFFFF 999999 999999 99999A	+3  0.3
  :                            50 .	00:00000C  C00000 666666 666666 666665	+4  -0.3
  :                            51 
  :                            52 .	In negative :
  :                            53 
  :                            54 .	0.5 and above	sign.midpoint = 1.0.discarded.retained : mantissa
  :                            55 .	< 0.5		sign.midpoint = 1.1.discarded.retained : mantissa
  :                            56 					
  :                            57 .	if the number is 0.5 or more midpoint magnitude is YES
  :                            58 .	the discarded exponent bits must = magnitude 0
  :                            59 
  :                            60 .		example +	400000 : mantissa
  :                            61 .		example -	BFFFFF : NOT_mantissa
  :                            62 
  :                            63 .	if the number value is < 0.5 midpoint magnitude is NO
  :                            64 .	the discarded exponent bits must = magnitude 1
  :                            65 .	to keep the < 1 exponent magnitude unchanged
  :                            66 
  :                            67 .		example	+	3FFFFF : mantissa
  :                            68 .		example	-	C00000 : NOT_mantissa
  :                            69 
  :                            70 .	negative floating values in RTA1 are 1s-complement :
  :                            71 .	all bits following the sign are represented XOR sign
  :                            72 
  :                            73 .	mantissa is all-fraction
  :                            74 .	without any imputed or unrepresented bit values
  :                            75 
  :                            76 .	unnornalised values compute as zero
  :                            77 
  :                            78 .	__fpx[D] are also called in sprintf format :
  :                            79 
  :                            80 .	__fpx on %lf %lg unpacks 48-bit floats of form s.11.36 for edit
  :                            81 .       __fpxD on %D %lD unpacks 48-bit floats of form s.15.32 for edit
  :                            82 .	from sprintf parameter stream
  :                            83 
  :                            84 .	alternatively 48-bit floats of format s.11.36
  :                            85 .	can be unpacked into the sprintf parameter stream
  :                            86 
  :                            87 .		item_name,,PACKED_FLOATING
  :                            88 
  :                            89 .	and then they are %f %g %qf %qg which all mean 96 bits
  :                            90 
  :                            91 .	sscanf on %lf calls __fpp to pack scanned parameters to form s.11.36
  :                            92 .	   and on %D calls __fppD to pack scanned parameters to form s.15.32
  :                            93 
  :                            94 __FORM_12_36	$equ	1
  :                            95 __FORM_16_32	$equ	1
  :                            96 
  :                            97 	$do	__FORM_16_32=0,fpx_D	. target of vector fpxD
